\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{1}(\operatorname{Gal}(L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))=\{1\}.

(3.4.9)

Let
L_{0}\subset L
be the maximal unramified subfield of
L
and consider the short exact sequence of Galois groups

\displaystyle 1\to I_{L}\to\operatorname{Gal}(L/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to\operatorname{Gal}(L_{0}/\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to 1.

(3.4.10)

Then we get an inflation restriction exact sequence of pointed sets

\displaystyle\cdots\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(\operatorname{Gal}(L_{0}/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{I_{L}})\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(\operatorname{Gal}(L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(I_{L},\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{O}_{L})).

(3.4.11)

The natural map
\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{O}_{L_{0}})\to\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{O}_{L})^{I_{L}}
is an isomorphism and so the first term is trivial by Lemma
3.4.2
. The assumption that
p
is tamely ramified in
\mathsf{F}
means that
I_{L}
has order prime to
p
and thus
\mathrm{H}^{1}(I_{L},\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))
vanishes by Lemma
3.4.4
and the fact that
\mathcal{H}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
is pro-
p
. It now follows from
[
31
, Corollary 1 on p. 52]
that
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\operatorname{Gal}(L/\mathbb{Q}_{p}),\mathcal{G}(\mathcal{O}_{L}))=\{1\}
.
∎

3.4.6.

The results proved above provide many compact open subgroups
K\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
that are
\Gamma
-stable and have
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K)=\{1\}
, at least when
\mathsf{F}
is tamely ramified over
\mathbb{Q}
.

Proposition 3.4.7
.

Suppose that
\mathsf{F}
is tamely ramified over
\mathbb{Q}
. Then the collection of compact open subgroups
K\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
that are
\Gamma
-stable and satisfy
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K)=\{1\}
is cofinal in the set of all compact open subgroups of
\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

We will call such compact open subgroups
good
, and we will use the same terminology for
\Gamma
-stable compact open subgroups
K\subset G(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{S})
, for some finite set of places
S
of
\mathbb{Q}
, that satisfy
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K)=\{1\}
.

Proof of Proposition
3.4.7
.

We can choose groups
K
of the form

\displaystyle K=\prod_{p}K_{p},

(3.4.12)

with
K_{p}=\mathcal{H}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
for
H=\operatorname{Res}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
for some smooth affine group scheme
\mathcal{G}
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
with connected special fiber and generic fiber
\mathsf{G}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, such that
\mathcal{G}
is a reductive model of
G
for all but finitely many
p
. This collection of compact open subgroups
K\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is cofinal by Lemma
3.4.1
. We can moreover assume that either
p
is unramified in
\mathsf{F}
or that
K_{p}
is a pro-
p
group, without affecting co-finality.

Then for primes
p
unramified in
\mathsf{F}
we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K_{p})=\{1\}

(3.4.13)

by Lemma
3.4.2
, and for primes
p
ramified in
\mathsf{F}
we have
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K_{p})=\{1\}
by Lemma
3.4.5
. Thus we find that

\displaystyle\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K)=\prod_{p}\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K_{p})=\{1\}

(3.4.14)

and the result is proved.
∎

3.5.
Homotopy fixed points of Shimura stacks

Let the notation be as in the beginning of Section
3
. If
K\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is a
\Gamma
-stable compact open subgroup then there is a natural morphism of groupoids (see Section
A.2.6
)

\displaystyle\left[\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\Gamma}\right)\right]\to\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\right)\right]^{h\Gamma}.

(3.5.1)

In this section we will investigate when this morphism is an equivalence.

Theorem 3.5.1
.

If
K\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is a good compact open subgroup and if
\Sha^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathsf{G})\to\Sha^{1}(\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G})
is injective, then the natural functor

\displaystyle\left[\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\Gamma}\right)\right]\to\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\right)\right]^{h\Gamma}

(3.5.2)

is an equivalence of groupoids.

It follows from the discussion in Section
3.1
that the assumption that
\Sha^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathsf{G})\to\Sha^{1}(\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G})
is injective is necessary. It follows from Section
3.1
that it is necessary that
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K)\to\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))
has trivial kernel.

Corollary 3.5.2
.

Suppose that the
\mathbb{R}
-split rank of the center of
\mathsf{G}
is zero. If
K\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is a neat and good compact open subgroup and
\Sha^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathsf{G})\to\Sha^{1}(\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G})
is injective, then the natural morphism of
\mathsf{E}
-varieties

\displaystyle\mathbf{Sh}_{K^{\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to\mathbf{Sh}_{K}{(\mathsf{H},\mathsf{Y})}^{\Gamma}

(3.5.3)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It suffices to prove this result after basechanging to
\mathbb{C}
. By
[
7
, Proposition 4.2]
, the target is smooth because
\mathbf{Sh}_{K}{(\mathsf{H},\mathsf{Y})}
is smooth. Therefore, it is enough to prove that the map induces a bijection on
\mathbb{C}
-points. Now recall from Lemma
3.2.2
that both
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
and
{(\mathsf{H},\mathsf{Y})}
satisfy SV5. Since
K
is neat it follows that
K^{\Gamma}=K\cap\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is neat and so by Lemma
3.2.1
, the groupoid quotients in the statement of Theorem
3.5.1
are equivalent to the set theoretic quotients. The corollary now follows from Theorem
3.5.1
.
∎

To prove Theorem
3.5.1
, it is more convenient to work with a different presentation of the morphisms of groupoids in equation (
3.5.1
). Informally, we want to swap around the order in which we are taking the quotient.
6
6

6

What follows is presumably a tautology for the readers well versed in
2
-category theory. We have opted to spell out the details for our own benefit.

A compact open subgroup
K\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
acts on
\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
by
k\cdot(x,g)=(x,g\cdot k)
. This action commutes with the action of
\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})
and therefore descends to an action of
K
on

\displaystyle\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})).

(3.5.4)

Moreover, if
K
is
\Gamma
-stable then this action is
\Gamma
-semilinear and thus induces an action of
\Gamma
on the quotient stack of
\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))
by
K
. There is moreover an action of
K^{\Gamma}
on
\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))
and the natural map

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))\to\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}))

(3.5.5)

induces a
\Gamma
-equivariant map of quotient stacks, see Section
A.2.6
. The following lemma tells us that we can indeed swap around the order in which the quotient is taken.

Lemma 3.5.3
.

The natural functor

\displaystyle\left[\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\Gamma}\right)\right]\to\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\right)\right]^{h\Gamma}

(3.5.6)

is an equivalence if and only if

\displaystyle\left[\left(\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\right)\right)/K^{\Gamma}\right]\to\left[\left(\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\right)\right)/K\right]^{h\Gamma}

(3.5.7)

is an equivalence.

Proof.

This is a straightforward consequence of Lemma
A.2.3
and equation
A.2.41
.
∎

Proof of Theorem
3.5.1
.

It follows from the assumptions of the theorem and Lemma
3.3.2
that the natural map

\displaystyle(\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\right)\to\left(\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\right)\right)^{\Gamma}

(3.5.8)

is a bijection. To show that the natural map

\displaystyle\left[\left(\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\right)\right)/K^{\Gamma}\right]\to\left[\left(\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\right)\right)/K\right]^{h\Gamma}

(3.5.9)

is an equivalence, it suffices by Corollary
A.2.13
to show that
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K)=\{1\}
, which is true by assumption. Therefore, the map in equation (
3.5.9
) is an equivalence and the theorem now follows from Lemma
3.5.3
.
∎

The following corollary is a direct consequence of the proof of Theorem
3.5.1
.

Corollary 3.5.4
.

If
K\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
is a
\Gamma
-stable subgroup (not necessarily compact open!) with
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K)=\{1\}
, then the natural map

\displaystyle\left[\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\Gamma}\right)\right]\to\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\left(\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K\right)\right]^{h\Gamma}

(3.5.10)

is an equivalence. In particular, the right-hand side is equivalent to a discrete groupoid if
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
satisfies SV5 and
K
is neat.

3.6.
Shimura varieties of Hodge type

Let the notation be as in the beginning of Section
3
. For a symplectic space
(V,\psi)
over
\mathbb{Q}
we write
\mathsf{G}_{V}:=\operatorname{GSp}(V,\psi)
for the group of symplectic similitudes of
V
over
\mathbb{Q}
. It admits a Shimura datum
\mathsf{H}_{V}
consisting of the union of the Siegel upper and lower half spaces. Assume furthermore that
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
is of Hodge type and let
\iota:{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to(\mathsf{G}_{V},\mathsf{H}_{V})
be a closed immersion of Shimura data. Let
\mathsf{F}
be a Galois totally real field with Galois group
\Gamma
.

Recall the following construction from
[
16
, Section 7.1.6]
7
7

7

They use
\mathbf{H}^{\prime}
for what we call
\mathsf{H}
and they use
\mathbf{H}
for what we call
\mathsf{H}_{1}
.
. Let
W
be the symplectic space
V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathsf{F}
considered as a vector space over
\mathbb{Q}
, equipped with the symplectic form
\psi_{W}
given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W\times W}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{F}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\psi\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathsf{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Tr}_{\mathsf{F}/\mathbb{Q}}}

(3.6.1)

Let
c_{\mathsf{G}}:\mathsf{G}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
be the restriction of the symplectic similitude character of
\mathsf{G}_{V}
to
\mathsf{G}
and let
c_{\mathsf{G},\mathsf{F}}:\mathsf{H}\to\operatorname{Res}_{\mathsf{F}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
be the induced map. Form the fiber product

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}_{3}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{G}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{Res}_{\mathsf{F}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{\mathsf{G},\mathsf{F}}}

(3.6.2)

and let
\mathsf{H}_{1}
be the neutral component of
\mathsf{H}_{3}
. Then
\mathsf{H}_{1}
is a connected reductive group over
\mathbb{Q}
and the natural map
\mathsf{G}\to\mathsf{H}
factors over
\mathsf{H}_{1}
. For
h:\mathbb{S}\to\mathsf{G}_{\mathbb{R}}
in
\mathsf{X}
we let
\mathsf{Y}_{1}
be the
\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{R})
-conjugacy class of the composition
h:\mathbb{S}\to\mathsf{G}_{\mathbb{R}}\to\mathsf{H}_{1,\mathbb{R}}
, this does not depend on the choice of
h
. We will write
\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}}
for the inverse image of
\mathbb{G}_{m}\subset\operatorname{Res}_{\mathsf{F}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}
under the natural map

\displaystyle\operatorname{Res}_{\mathsf{F}/\mathbb{Q}}G_{V}\to\operatorname{Res}_{\mathsf{F}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathbb{G}_{m}.

(3.6.3)

As explained above, this also comes equipped with a Shimura datum
\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}}
. There is moreover a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}_{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{G}_{W},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{\mathsf{H}_{1}}}

(3.6.4)

where each map is a closed immersion compatible with the natural Shimura data.

Remark 3.6.1
.

The group
\mathsf{H}_{1}
does not depend on the choice of
\iota
, since the map
c_{\mathsf{G}}
does not depend on the choice of
\iota
, see
[
3
, Lemma 7.1.1]
.

3.6.2.

Shimura varieties of Hodge type automatically satisfy SV5 and in fact their centers have
\mathbb{Q}
-split and
\mathbb{R}
-split ranks equal to
1
. Recall from Lemma
3.2.2
that if
[\mathsf{F}:\mathbb{Q}]>1
then
{(\mathsf{H},\mathsf{Y})}
does not satisfy SV5. Since
{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
is of Hodge type, it does satisfy SV5. The point of the construction of
\mathsf{H}_{1}
is both to remedy the failure of SV5 and to build something that is again of Hodge type.

We would like to compare the
\Gamma
-fixed points of Shimura varieties for
{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
to the Shimura varieties for
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
. It seems complicated to do this directly, so we will instead compare the
\mathbb{C}
-points of the
\Gamma
-fixed points of the Shimura varieties for
{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
, to the
\Gamma
-homotopy fixed points of the Shimura stacks for
{(\mathsf{H},\mathsf{Y})}
. We will need the following modification of
[
17
, Lemma 2.1.2]
, cf.
[
20
, Lemma 2.4.3]
.

Proposition 3.6.3
.

Let
\ell
be a prime number coprime to the order of
\Gamma
. Let
K^{\ell}\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\ell})
be a neat good compact open subgroup and let
K_{\ell}\subset\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
be a
\Gamma
-stable pro-
\ell
group. Define
K=K^{\ell}K_{\ell}
and define
K_{1,\ell}=K_{\ell}\cap\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})
and
K^{1,\ell}=K^{\ell}\cap\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\ell})
. Then the natural map

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}^{\ell}K_{1,\ell}\right)^{\Gamma}\to\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}K_{1,\ell}\right]^{h\Gamma}

(3.6.5)

is fully faithful.
8
8

8

The groupoid on the right hand side is equivalent to a discrete groupoid by Corollary
3.5.4
, and thus the lemma is equivalent to saying that the map on isomorphism classes of objects is injective.

Proof of Proposition
3.6.3
.

We first prove that the natural functor

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}^{\ell}\right)^{\Gamma}\to\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}\right]^{h\Gamma}

(3.6.6)

is fully faithful. For this we consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}\right]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}.}

(3.6.7)

The proof of
[
20
, Lemma 2.4.3]
establishes that the diagonal arrow is injective: Indeed, we consider the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash H(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}).}

(3.6.8)

The bottom arrow is injective since
\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\cap H_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell})=\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})
. The fibers of the vertical maps can be identified with
\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\ell})/K_{1}^{\ell}
and
\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\ell})/K^{\ell}
, respectively, and the natural map between them is injective since
K_{1}^{\ell}=K^{\ell}\cap\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{\ell})
. We conclude that the top arrow is injective, and it follows that the diagonal arrow in the following diagram is injective

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}^{\ell}\right)^{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}\right]^{h\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}\right)^{\Gamma}.}

(3.6.9)

Since
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K^{\ell})=\{1\}
by assumption, it follows from Corollary
3.5.4
that

\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}\right]^{h\Gamma}

is a discrete groupoid. Therefore
\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}^{\ell}\right)^{\Gamma}\to\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}\right]^{h\Gamma}
must be a fully faithful map of discrete groupoids. To continue the proof we consider the
2
-commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}^{\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}\right]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}^{\ell}K_{1,\ell}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}K_{1,\ell}\right],}

(3.6.10)

which we note is
2
-Cartesian since the vertical arrows are essentially surjective with fibers given by the discrete groupoid associated to
K_{1,\ell}
. By Lemma
A.1.11
, it follows that the diagram stays Cartesian when applying
\Gamma
-homotopy fixed points. Since
K_{\ell}
is pro-
\ell
by assumption, it follows that
K_{1,\ell}
is also pro-
\ell
and thus
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K_{1,\ell})=\{1\}
by Lemma
3.4.4
. Therefore, it follows from Corollary
A.2.13
that the left vertical map in the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}^{\ell}\right)^{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}\right]^{h\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}^{\ell}K_{1,\ell}\right)^{\Gamma}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}K_{1,\ell}\right],}

(3.6.11)

is (essentially) surjective, and fully faithfullness of the top row therefore implies the fully faithfulness of the bottom row.
∎

Theorem 3.6.4
.

Let
K\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
be a neat and good compact open subgroup. If there is a prime number
\ell
coprime to the order of
\Gamma
such that
K=K^{\ell}K_{\ell}
with
K_{\ell}
a pro-
\ell
group, and if
\Sha^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathsf{G})\to\Sha^{1}(\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G})
is injective, then the natural map

\displaystyle\mathbf{Sh}_{K^{\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to\mathbf{Sh}_{K_{1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}

(3.6.12)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

As in the proof of Corollary
3.5.2
, we reduce to showing that the map is a bijection on
\mathbb{C}
-points. This map has the form

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\Gamma}\to\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}\right)^{\Gamma}

(3.6.13)

and we consider the composition

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\to\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1})\right)^{\Gamma}

(3.6.14)

\displaystyle\to\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}K_{1,\ell})\right]^{h\Gamma}.

(3.6.15)

Let
\ell
and
K_{\ell}
be as in the statement of the theorem. Since
K_{\ell}
is pro-
\ell
by assumption, it follows that
K_{1,\ell}
is also pro-
\ell
and thus
\mathrm{H}^{1}(\Gamma,K_{1,\ell})=\{1\}
by Lemma
3.4.4
. Therefore it follows from Corollary
3.5.4
that this composition is an equivalence of categories. By Proposition
3.6.3
, the map

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1}^{\ell}K_{1,\ell}\right)^{\Gamma}\to\left[\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\ell}K_{1,\ell}\right]^{h\Gamma}

(3.6.16)

is fully faithful and thus it follows that

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K^{\Gamma}

\displaystyle\to\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash(\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})/K_{1})\right)^{\Gamma}

(3.6.17)

is an equivalence, since its composition with a fully faithful map is an equivalence. Since an equivalence of categories between discrete categories gives a bijection of the underlying sets, we are done.
∎

Remark 3.6.5
.

Our original approach to proving this theorem was to directly study the
\Gamma
-fixed points of the Shimura variety for
{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
using non-abelian cohomology; this calculation proved quite difficult. This is why it is useful to consider homotopy fixed points of Shimura stacks, even if one is just interested in studying Shimura varieties of Hodge type.

3.6.6.

Finally, we collect a result for use in Section
6
. For
K\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
a
\Gamma
-stable compact open subgroup we will consider
K^{\Gamma}\subset\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
and
K_{1}\subset\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
.

Lemma 3.6.7
.

If
\Sha^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathsf{G})\to\Sha^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathsf{H})
is injective, then the natural map of
\mathsf{E}
-schemes

\displaystyle\kappa:\varprojlim_{K\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})}\mathbf{Sh}_{K^{\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to\left(\varprojlim_{K\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})}\mathbf{Sh}_{K_{1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}\right)^{\Gamma},

(3.6.19)

where the limit runs over
\Gamma
-stable compact open subgroups, is an isomorphism.

Proof.

It suffices to prove this after basechanging to
\mathbb{C}
. To prove it is an isomorphism over
\mathbb{C}
, we first show it induces a bijection on
\mathbb{C}
-points. There we are looking at the map (injective by
[
6
, Variante 1.15.1]
)

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\right)^{\Gamma}.

(3.6.20)

As before we consider the composition

\displaystyle\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{X}\times\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\to\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\right)^{\Gamma}\to\left(\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\right)^{\Gamma},

(3.6.21)

which is an isomorphism by Lemma
3.3.2
. It now suffices to show that

\displaystyle\left(\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}_{1}\times\mathsf{H}_{1}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\right)^{\Gamma}\to\left(\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{Q})\backslash\mathsf{Y}\times\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f})\right)^{\Gamma}

(3.6.22)

is injective, which can be checked before taking
\Gamma
-fixed points. But then the result is well known, see
[
6
, Variante 1.15.1]
.

To deduce that
\kappa
is an isomorphism, we argue as follows: It follows from the proof of
[
6
, Proposition 1.15]
that
\kappa
is a closed immersion, thus it suffices to show the map is surjective on topological spaces. Both the source and target are Jacobson schemes since they are integral over the bottom object of the inverse limit, which is Jacobson; thus
\mathbb{C}
-points are dense in the source and target. Furthermore, the map
\kappa
is integral since it is an inverse limit of integral maps of schemes. Therefore
\kappa
is universally closed, and thus surjective since the image is closed and contains the dense set of
\mathbb{C}
-points.
∎

4.
Integral models of Shimura varieties

In this section we prove some results about integral models of Shimura varieties of Hodge type that we will need, in particular some
\Gamma
-equivariance properties for the integral models for
{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
. In Section
4.1
we prove that the association of the universal local shtuka to a Shimura variety is functorial in
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})}
in the two-categorical sense, which we use to deduce
\Gamma
-equivariance in the two-categorical sense. This is transferred to shtukas on integral models in Section
4
. In Section
4.3
, we prove that certain
\Gamma
-equivariant morphisms of Shimura varieties can be upgraded to
\Gamma
-equivariant morphisms of the corresponding local model diagrams.

4.1.
Shtukas on Shimura varieties

Let
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
be a Shimura datum with reflex field
\mathsf{E}
satisfying Milne’s axiom SV5 from
[
23
, p. 64]
. Fix a prime
p
and write
G=\mathsf{G}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Fix a prime
v
of
\mathsf{E}
above
p
and let
E
be the
v
-adic completion of
\mathsf{E}
with ring of integers
\mathcal{O}_{E}
and residue field
k_{E}
. Let
\mu
be the
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-conjugacy class of cocharacters of
G
coming from the Hodge cocharacter of some
x\in\mathsf{X}
and the choice of place
v
.

Let
\mathcal{G}
be a quasi-parahoric model of
G
over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and set
\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p}):=K_{p}\subset G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. For
K^{p}\subset\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f})
a neat compact open subgroup we write
K=K^{p}K_{p}
and consider the Shimura variety
\mathbf{Sh}_{K}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
over
E
. The group
\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
acts on the inverse limit

\displaystyle\mathbf{Sh}_{K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}:=\varprojlim_{K^{p}}\mathbf{Sh}_{K^{p}K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}.

(4.1.1)

If it is clear from context, we will omit
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
from the notation. By
[
24
, Proposition 4.1.2]
and
[
4
, Section 4.1.1]
, there are morphisms

\displaystyle\mathbf{Sh}_{K}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}^{\lozenge}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\otimes_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\operatorname{Spd}E

(4.1.2)

that are compatible with changing
K^{p}
. The goal of this section is to investigate the functoriality of this construction in the triple
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})}
.

4.1.1.

Consider the category
\operatorname{ShTrp}
whose objects are triples
(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})
, where
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
is a Shimura datum satisfying SV5 and where
\mathcal{G}
is a quasi-parahoric model of
G
. Morphisms in
\operatorname{ShTrp}
are morphisms
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to(\mathsf{G}^{\prime},\mathsf{X}^{\prime})
that extend (necessarily uniquely) to
\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. For
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})}\in\operatorname{ShTrp}
, we will write
K_{p}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. If we fix an isomorphism
\mathbb{C}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
, then the reflex field
\mathsf{E}
of each Shimura datum has a natural embedding
\mathsf{E}\to\mathbb{C}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
with completion
E
. For
L\subset\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
a finite extension of
\mathbb{Q}_{p}
, we will write
\operatorname{ShTrp}_{L}\subset\operatorname{ShTrp}
for the full subcategory of triples such that the reflex field is contained in
L
. There is a strict functor

\displaystyle\mathbf{Sh}:\operatorname{ShTrp}_{L}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{D}_{L}

(4.1.3)

\displaystyle(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\mapsto\mathbf{Sh}_{K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}_{L}^{\lozenge},

(4.1.4)

and similarly a strict functor sending

\displaystyle\mathbf{Sh}_{\infty}:(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})

\displaystyle\mapsto\varprojlim_{U_{p}}\mathbf{Sh}_{U_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}_{L}^{\lozenge}.

(4.1.5)

We also get a strict functor sending
(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})\mapsto\underline{K_{p}}
and thus a weak functor
\mathbb{B}
sending
(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})\mapsto\mathbb{B}\underline{K_{p}}
, see Section
A.2.6
. The natural map

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{U_{p}}\mathbf{Sh}_{U_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}^{\lozenge}\to\mathbf{Sh}_{K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}^{\lozenge}

(4.1.6)

is a
\underline{K}_{p}
-torsor and corresponds to a weak natural transformation (see Lemma
A.2.3
)

\displaystyle\mathbf{Sh}\to\mathbb{B}.

(4.1.7)

4.1.2.

There is a natural functor
\operatorname{ShTrp}_{L}\to\operatorname{ShtPr}_{L}
sending
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})}\mapsto(\mathcal{G},\mu)
, where
\mu
is the
G(\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p})
-conjugacy class of cocharacters of
G
corresponding to our fixed isomorphism
\mathbb{C}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
. Thus we can think of
\operatorname{Sht}:\operatorname{ShtPr}_{L}\to\mathcal{D}_{L}
as a weak functor on
\operatorname{ShTrp}_{L}
. We let
(\mathbb{B}\underline{K_{p}})^{\mathrm{\mathrm{dR}}}
denote the sub (pre-)stack of
(\mathbb{B}\underline{K_{p}})
consisting of
K_{p}
-torsors that are de-Rham in the sense of
[
24
, Definition 2.6.1]
.

Lemma 4.1.3
.

The morphism
\mathbf{Sh}_{K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}_{L}^{\lozenge}\to\mathbb{B}\underline{K_{p}}
factors through
(\mathbb{B}\underline{K_{p}})^{\mathrm{\mathrm{dR}}}\subset(\mathbb{B}\underline{K_{p}})
.

Proof.

This follows from the main results of
[
19
]
, as explained in the proof of
[
24
, Proposition 4.1.2]
.
∎

Proposition 4.1.4
.

There is a weak natural transformation
\mathbf{Sh}\to\operatorname{Sht}
factoring on objects through
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,L}
, which recovers the construction of
[
24
, Section 2.6]
for each triple
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})}
.

Proof.

We are going to chain together a number of weak functors below. In the rest of this proof we let
S=\mathbf{Sh}_{K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}_{L}^{\lozenge}
.

There is a functor from
(\mathbb{B}\underline{K_{p}})(S)
to the groupoid of exact tensor functors
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{G}\to\{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\text{ local systems on }S\}
, see
[
24
, Section 2.6.2]
. It is given by sending a
\underline{K_{p}}
-torsor
\mathcal{P}\to S
to the tensor functor sending a representation
\rho:\mathcal{G}\to\operatorname{GL}(\Lambda)
, where
\Lambda
is a finite free
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-module, to the
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-local system

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}\times^{\rho}\underline{\Lambda}.

(4.1.8)

This is weakly functorial in
f:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
by using the identifications

\displaystyle\left(\mathcal{P}\times^{f}\mathcal{G^{\prime}}\right)\times^{\rho^{\prime}}\underline{\Lambda}\to\mathcal{P}\times^{\rho}\underline{\Lambda}

(4.1.9)

of Section
A.2
. Here
\rho^{\prime}:\mathcal{G}^{\prime}\to\operatorname{GL}(\Lambda)
is a representation whose composition with
f
gives the representation
\rho
of
\mathcal{G}
. Per definition of de-Rham local systems, this equivalence identifies
(\mathbb{B}\underline{K_{p}})^{\mathrm{\mathrm{dR}}}(S)
with the groupoid of exact tensor functors
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{G}\to\{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\text{ de-Rham local systems on }S\}
.

Pappas and Rapoport construct in
[
24
, Proposition 2.6.3, Definition 2.6.4]
an equivalence of categories

\displaystyle\{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\text{ de-Rham local systems on }S\}\to\{\text{Shtukas on }S\}

(4.1.10)

compatible with tensor products. Composing with this equivalence gives us a functor from the groupoid of exact tensor functors

\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{G}\to\{\mathbb{Z}_{p}\text{ de-Rham local systems on }S\}

to the groupoid of exact tensor functors
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{G}\to\{\text{Shtukas on }S\}
, which is weakly functorial in
f:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
.

Last, we claim that there is an equivalence of categories between the groupoid of exact tensor functors
\operatorname{Rep}_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{G}\to\{\text{shtukas over }S\}
to
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}}(S)
, which is weakly functorial in
\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}^{\prime}
. The equivalence is explained in
[
10
, Section 5.1.1]
, and the weak functoriality can be proved in the same way as in the first paragraph of this proof.
∎

4.2.
The Pappas–Rapoport axioms

Before we can state the Pappas–Rapoport axioms we introduce some notation. Given a formal scheme
\mathcal{X}\to\operatorname{Spf}\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
that is formally locally of finite type, and a closed point
x\in\mathcal{X}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
, we denote by
\widehat{\mathcal{X}}_{/x}
, the
formal neighborhood
of
\mathcal{X}
at
x
. There is a similar construction for certain v-sheaves over
\operatorname{Spd}\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
, see
[
24
, Section 3.3.1]
. We will only use this for the tautological point
x_{0}\in\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G},b,\mu}(\operatorname{Spd}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
.

Pappas and Rapoport conjecture, see
[
24
, Conjecture 4.2.2]
(and
[
4
, Conjecture 4.1.4]
in the quasi-parahoric case), that there are flat normal integral models
\mathscr{S}_{K}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{E}
of
\mathbf{Sh}_{K}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
for sufficiently small
K^{p}
, together with forgetful maps
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\prime p}K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to\mathscr{S}_{K}
over
\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{E}
extending the ones on the generic fiber, such that:

a)

The forgetful morphisms are finite étale and
\mathsf{G}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
acts on the inverse limit

\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}:=\varprojlim_{K^{p}}\mathscr{S}_{K^{p}K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}.

Moreover, for every discrete valuation ring
R
over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
with characteristic
(0,p)
the natural map

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}(R)\to\mathbf{Sh}_{K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}(R[1/p])

(4.2.1)

is a bijection.

b)

For all sufficiently small
K^{p}
the map
\mathbf{Sh}_{K}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}^{\lozenge}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\otimes_{\operatorname{Spd}\mathcal{O}_{E}}\operatorname{Spd}E
extends to a map

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{K}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}^{\lozenge/}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}.

(4.2.2)

c)

For all sufficiently small
K^{p}
and every
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
with induced
b_{x}:\operatorname{Spd}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
, there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\Theta_{x}:\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G},b_{x},\mu,/x_{0}}\to\left(\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K,/x}}\right)^{\lozenge},

(4.2.3)

such that: The pullback under
\Theta_{x}
of the
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka over
\mathscr{S}_{K}^{\lozenge/}
coming from (
4.2.2
), is isomorphic to the tautological
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka over
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G},b_{x},\mu,/x_{0}}
.

By
[
4
, Remark 4.1.5]
, the morphism in b) automatically factors through the open and closed substack
\mathrm{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu,\delta=1}\subset\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
.

4.2.1.

We will refer to integral models satisfying their conjecture as integral models satisfying the Pappas–Rapoport axioms or as
integral canonical models
. Pappas and Rapoport prove, see
[
24
, Theorem 4.2.4]
, that such integral models are unique if they exist (see
[
4
, Theorem 4.1.8]
for the quasi-parahoric case). These integral models are moreover functorial in the triple
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})}
, see
[
4
, Corollary 4.1.10]
. By
[
4
, Theorem I]
, integral models satisfying their conjecture exist if
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
is of Hodge type.

Let us denote by
\operatorname{ShTrp}_{L,\operatorname{SV5},\mathrm{PR}}
the category of triples
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})}
such that
\mathsf{E}\to\mathbb{C}\to\overline{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
factors through
L
, such that
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
satisfies SV5 and such that
[
4
, Conjecture 4.1.4]
holds for
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})}
. Then as explained above, there is a strict functor
\mathscr{S}^{\lozenge/}:\operatorname{ShTrp}_{L,\operatorname{SV5},\mathrm{PR}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\circ}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}
sending
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})}
to
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}^{\lozenge/}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}
. We can now state an important corollary to Proposition
4.1.4
.

Corollary 4.2.2
.

There is a weak natural transformation
\mathscr{S}\to\operatorname{Sht}
of weak functors
\operatorname{ShTrp}_{L,\operatorname{SV5},\mathrm{PR}}\to\mathcal{D}^{\circ}_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}
, with underlying
1
-morphisms the morphisms

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}^{\lozenge/}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}

(4.2.4)

guaranteed to exist by axiom b).

Proof.

Restricting to the generic fiber gives a fully faithful functor

\displaystyle\operatorname{Hom}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}^{\lozenge/},\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu})\to\operatorname{Hom}(\mathbf{Sh}_{K_{p}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}^{\lozenge},\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}),

(4.2.5)

by
[
24
, Corollary 2.7.10]
. To turn the collection of
1
-morphisms specified in the lemma into a weak functor, we have to specify certain coherence isomorphisms satisfying certain properties, see Definition
A.1.1
. By the fully faithfulness, it suffices to do this on the generic fiber, where the result is Proposition
4.1.4
.
∎

4.2.3.

Now we prove some uniqueness results for the maps in axiom c).

Lemma 4.2.4
.

Let
R
be a complete Noetherian local
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebra equipped with a morphism of
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebras
x:R\to\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
and let
(\operatorname{Spf}R)^{\lozenge}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
be a morphism. Let
b_{x}:\operatorname{Spd}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
be the
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka over
\operatorname{Spd}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
induced by
x
. Then there is a unique morphism

\displaystyle\Psi_{b_{x}}:(\operatorname{Spf}R)^{\lozenge}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G},b_{x},\mu,/x_{0}}

(4.2.6)

such that
\operatorname{Spd}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}\to(\operatorname{Spf}R)^{\lozenge}\xrightarrow{\beta}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G},b_{x},\mu,/x_{0}}\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
is equal to
b_{x}
.

Proof.

The uniqueness is a direct consequence of
[
24
, Proposition 4.2.5]
, and the existence is
[
24
, Proposition 4.7.1]
. [Here we note that the proof of the latter also works when
\mathcal{G}
is quasi-parahoric by using
[
25
, Proposition 4.2.1]
to transport the statement about formal neighborhoods to the quasi-parahoric case.]
∎

Corollary 4.2.5
.

Let
\mathscr{S}_{K}=\mathscr{S}_{K}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
be an integral model satisfying axioms a),b),c) above and let
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
. Let
b_{x}:\operatorname{Spd}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}
denote the induced
\mathcal{G}
-shtuka over
\operatorname{Spd}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
. Then the map

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K,/x}}\right)^{\lozenge}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G},b^{\prime},\mu,/x_{0}}

(4.2.7)

of Lemma
4.2.4
is an isomorphism

Proof.

This is a straightforward consequence of axiom c) and the uniqueness proved in Lemma
4.2.4
.
∎

4.3.
Local model diagrams

The purpose of this section is to discuss local model diagrams for the integral models of Shimura varieties constructed by Kisin–Zhou. In particular, we want to show that these local model diagrams are
\Gamma
-equivariant.

4.3.1.

Let
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
be a Shimura datum of Hodge type with reflex field
\mathsf{E}
, let
p>2
be a prime such that
p
is coprime to the order of
\pi_{1}(G^{\mathrm{der}})
and let
G=\mathsf{G}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
as usual.

Hypothesis 4.3.2
.

Either
G
splits over a tamely ramified extension, or there is a Hodge embedding
\iota:{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to(\mathsf{G}_{V},\mathsf{H}_{V})
such that the identity component
\mathsf{G}^{\mathrm{Sp}}
of
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\cap\mathsf{Sp}_{V}
is isomorphic to

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{s}\operatorname{Res}_{\mathsf{K}_{i}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathsf{H}_{i}^{\mathrm{Sp}},

(4.3.1)

where
\mathsf{K}_{1},\cdots,\mathsf{K}_{s}
are totally real fields and where
\mathsf{H}_{i}^{\mathrm{Sp}}
is a connected reductive group over
\mathsf{K}_{i}
whose base change to each
p
-adic place of
\mathsf{K}_{i}
is tamely ramified.

4.3.3.

Let
\mathcal{G}:=\mathcal{G}_{x}
be the stabilizer Bruhat–Tits group scheme (also called stabilizer quasi-parahoric, see
[
25
, Section 2.2]
) associated to a point
x
in the Bruhat–Tits building of
G
. Let
v
be a place of
\mathsf{E}
above
p
and let
E
be the
v
-adic completion of
\mathsf{E}
.

Assume that Hypothesis
4.3.2
holds and let
\iota
be the Hodge embedding guaranteed to exist by that hypothesis. After possibly replacing
\iota
by another Hodge embedding and
(V,\psi)
by another symplectic space over
\mathbb{Q}
, we may assume that: The Hodge embedding
\iota
is good for
(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})
with respect to some lattice
\Lambda\subset V\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
in the sense of
[
16
, Section 5.1.2]
, and
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
satisfies Hypothesis
4.3.2
. Indeed, this follows from
[
16
, Lemma 5.1.3]
, noting that the new Hodge embedding
\iota^{\prime}
constructed from
\iota
in the proof of
[
16
, Proposition 3.3.18]
still satisfies the condition above in terms of
\mathsf{G}^{\mathrm{SP}}
. Then
\mathcal{G}
embeds into the parahoric group scheme
\mathcal{G}_{V}
of
G_{V}
that is the stabilizer of the lattice
\Lambda
.

4.3.4.

Let
\mathsf{F}
be a Galois totally real field extension of
\mathbb{Q}
unramified at
p
, let
\Gamma
be its Galois group and let
F=\mathsf{F}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
. Let
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}\to{(\mathsf{H},\mathsf{Y})}
and
\iota_{H_{1}}:{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}\to(\mathsf{G}_{W},\mathsf{H}_{W})
be as in Section
3.6
, where we recall that
W=V\otimes_{\mathbb{Q}}\mathsf{F}
. Let
\mathcal{H}_{1}
denote the Bruhat–Tits stabilizer group scheme associated to the image of
x
under the morphism of buildings
B(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p})\to B(H_{1},\mathbb{Q}_{p})
, and let
\mathcal{H}=\operatorname{Res}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{G}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}
be the Bruhat–Tits stabilizer scheme associated to the image of
x
in
B(H,\mathbb{Q}_{p})
.

Recall from
[
16
, Section 2.4.8]
that there is a natural map
\beta:\mathcal{H}_{1}\to\mathcal{H}
.

Lemma 4.3.5
.

If Hypothesis
4.3.2
holds, then the natural map
\mathcal{H}_{1}\to\mathcal{H}
is a closed immersion.

Proof.

By
[
16
, Proposition 2.4.9]
, it suffices to show that the centraliser
T_{1}
of a maximal
\breve{\mathbb{Q}}_{p}
-split torus in
H_{1}
is an
R
-smooth torus (in the sense of
[
16
, Section 5.1.2]
). This is automatic when
G
is tamely ramified, see
[
16
, Proposition 2.4.6]
. Otherwise, we observe that

\displaystyle H_{1}^{\mathrm{Sp}}=\operatorname{Res}_{\mathsf{F}/\mathbb{Q}}\left(\operatorname{Res}_{\mathsf{K}_{i}/\mathbb{Q}}\mathsf{H}_{i}^{\mathrm{Sp}}\right)_{\mathsf{F}}

(4.3.2)

and this implies as in the proof of
[
16
, Proposition 5.2.6]
that
T_{1}
is
R
-smooth.
∎

4.3.6.

We recall the following commutative diagram of closed embeddings of Shimura data from Section
3.6

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{G}_{V},\mathsf{H}_{V})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathsf{G}_{W},\mathsf{H}_{W}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{H_{1}}}

(4.3.3)

Let
\mathcal{G}_{W}
be the parahoric group scheme of
G_{W}=\mathsf{G}_{W}\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
that is the stabilizer of the lattice
\Lambda_{W}=\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{F}
and let
\mathcal{GL}_{W}
be the reductive integral model of
\operatorname{GL}_{W}
corresponding to
\Lambda_{W}
. We will also consider the parahoric model
\mathcal{G}_{V,F}
of
\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}}
corresponding to
\Lambda\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}=:\Lambda_{F}
(thought of as an
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-module). We will write
M_{p,F}=\mathcal{G}_{V,F}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
M_{p,W}=\mathcal{G}_{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Lemma 4.3.7
.

The morphism
\iota_{H_{1}}
is a good Hodge embedding with respect to the lattice
\Lambda_{W}=\Lambda\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{O}_{F}\subset W\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{p}
.

Proof.

We need to check that the morphism
\iota_{H_{1}}
satisfies the following three properties

(i)

\iota_{H_{1}}(H_{1})
contains the scalars.

(ii)

\iota_{H_{1}}
extends to a closed immersion
\iota_{H_{1}}:\mathcal{H}_{1}\to\mathcal{GL}_{W}
.

(iii)

The natural map of local models
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mu}\to\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{GL}_{W},\mu}
induced by
\mathcal{H}_{1}\to\mathcal{GL}_{W}
is a closed immersion.
9
9

9

This condition is equivalent to condition (3) of
[
16
, Definition 3.3.15]
because there is a unique map extending the natural map on the generic fiber. Moreover, the local models used by
[
16
]
agree with ours because theirs also satisfy the Scholze–Weinstein conjecture (which means that they have the correct associated v-sheaf), see
[
16
, Proposition 3.3.10]
.

We know that
\iota_{H_{1}}(H_{1})
contains the scalars because
\iota(\mathsf{G})
contains the scalars of
G_{V}
and the image of the natural map
G_{V,F}\to G_{W}
contains the scalars. Property (ii) is a consequence of Lemma
4.3.5
together with the fact that
\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{GL}_{W}
is a closed immersion because it is the restriction of scalars along
\operatorname{Spec}\mathcal{O}_{F}\to\operatorname{Spec}\mathbb{Z}_{p}
of (the base change to
\mathcal{O}_{F}
of) the closed immersion
\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{GL}_{V}
. Property (iii) follows because under our assumptions we have functorial isomorphism (see Lemma
2.4.4
)

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{GL}_{W},\mu}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}

\displaystyle\simeq\prod_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{GL}_{V},\mu}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}

(4.3.4)

\displaystyle\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mu}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}\simeq\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H},\mu}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}

\displaystyle\simeq\prod_{i=1}^{d}\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}

(4.3.5)

and property (iii) holds for
\iota:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}_{V}
by assumption.
∎

4.3.8.

We let
K_{p}=\mathcal{H}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
which gives
K_{p,1}=\mathcal{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
K_{p}^{\Gamma}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. We recall the construction of
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p,1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
and
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}^{\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
from
[
16
, Section 5.1.4]
, and note that they come with natural maps

\displaystyle\iota:\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}^{\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{S}_{M_{p}}(\mathsf{G}_{V},\mathsf{H}_{V})

(4.3.6)

\displaystyle\iota_{H_{1}}:\mathscr{S}_{K_{p,1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}

\displaystyle\to\mathscr{S}_{M_{p,W}}(\mathsf{G}_{W},\mathsf{H}_{W}),

(4.3.7)

where
M_{p}=\mathcal{G}_{V}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
M_{p,W}=\mathcal{G}_{W}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. The targets of these morphisms have a moduli interpretation in terms of (weakly) polarized abelian schemes
(A,\lambda)
up to prime-to-
p
isogeny with prime-to-
p
level structures. By
[
16
, Proposition 5.2.2]
, the
\mathcal{O}_{E}
scheme
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}^{\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
fits in a local model diagram

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathscr{S}}_{K_{p}^{\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi}
\scriptstyle{q}
\textstyle{\mathscr{S}_{K_{p}^{\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu},}

where
q
is a
\mathcal{G}
-torsor and
\pi
is pro-smooth of relative dimension
\dim G
. We have a similar local model diagram for
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p,1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
given by

\textstyle{\widetilde{\mathscr{S}}_{K_{p,1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{q_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathscr{S}_{K_{p,1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}}
\textstyle{\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H},\mu},}

where
q_{1}
is an
\mathcal{H}_{1}
-torsor and
\pi_{1}
is pro-smooth of relative dimension
\dim H_{1}
.

Remark 4.3.9
.

The map
\pi
has an entirely classical description over the generic fiber, see
[
13
, Proposition 4.2.26]
. The torsor of trivializations of the first de-Rham cohomology of the universal abelian variety over
\mathscr{S}_{M_{p}}(\mathsf{G}_{V},\mathsf{H}_{V})
has a natural reduction to a
\mathcal{G}
-bundle
\mathcal{V}_{\mathrm{dR}}
. The morphism
\pi
is given by assigning to a trivialization
\phi
of the first de-Rham cohomology, the pre-image
\phi^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{1})
of the nontrivial step of the Hodge filtration.

4.3.10.

We require the following results on the local model diagram.

Proposition 4.3.11
.

(1)

There exists a
\Gamma
-action on
\widetilde{\mathscr{S}}_{K_{p,1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
extending that on
\mathscr{S}_{K_{p,1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
, which fits in a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathscr{S}}_{K_{p,1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathscr{S}}_{M_{p,F}}(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{K_{p,1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathscr{S}_{M_{p,F}}(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H},\mu}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}_{V,F},\mu},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\iota}_{H_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{F,V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{F,V}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{H_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{H_{1},p}}

(4.3.8)

and a
\Gamma
-action on
\widetilde{\mathscr{S}}_{M_{p,F}}(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})
such that
\pi_{1},\pi_{F,V},q_{1},q_{F,V},\iota_{H_{1},p},\iota_{H_{1}}
are
\Gamma
-equivariant.

Proof.

To prove the Proposition it suffices to consider the case
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}=(\mathsf{G}_{V},\mathsf{H}_{V})
, whence
{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}=(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})
. Indeed, we already know that the
\Gamma
-action on
\mathbf{Sh}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
extends to
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
by
[
4
, Corollary 4.1.10]
. Thus we are only trying to show that the action extends to the
\mathcal{H}_{1}
-torsor, and this admits a closed immersion into the pullback of
\widetilde{\mathscr{S}}_{M_{p,F}}(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})\to\mathscr{S}_{M_{p,F}}(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})
along the
\Gamma
-equivariant natural map
\iota_{H_{1}}
. Moreover, the
\Gamma
-equivariance of the induced maps can be checked on the generic fiber and then over
\mathbb{C}
, where it is clear from uniformization.

We may also assume that
\Lambda\subset V
is a self-dual
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-lattice whence we may assume the same for
\Lambda_{W}\subset V_{F}
. To define the action of
\Gamma
on the torsor
\widetilde{\mathscr{S}}_{M_{p,F}}(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})
we may make use of the moduli problem associated to this torsor. Recall that
\mathscr{S}_{M_{p,F}}(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})
classifies, over
S
a
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
-scheme, tuples
(A,\lambda,i,\overline{\eta})
where:

(1)

h:A\to S
is an abelian scheme of dimension
[\mathsf{F}:\mathbb{Q}]\cdot\text{dim}(V)/2
considered up to prime-to-p isogeny.

(2)

\lambda
is a
\mathbb{Z}_{(p)}^{\times}
-multiple of a principal polarization.

(3)

i:\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{F},(p)}\to\operatorname{End}_{S}(A)
is a map of rings such that the Rosati involution associated to
\lambda
induces the identity on
\mathcal{O}_{F}
and such that
i
satisfies a determinant condition as in
[
18
, Section 5]
.

(4)

\eta
is a trivialization
\eta:V^{p}(A)\xrightarrow{\sim}V_{F}\otimes\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p}
compatible with the
\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{F},(p)}
-action and with the polarization up to a scalar in
\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p,\times}
.

The scheme
\widetilde{\mathscr{S}}_{M_{p,F}}(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})
classifies quintuples
(A,\lambda,i,\eta,\tau)
where
(A,\lambda,i,\eta)
are as above and
\tau:\Lambda^{\vee}_{F}\xrightarrow{\sim}R^{1}h_{*}\Omega_{A/S}^{*}
is compatible with the symplectic pairing and
\mathcal{O}_{F}
actions on the source and target. The
\Gamma
-action on the moduli problem
\widetilde{\mathscr{S}_{M_{p,F}}}(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})
sends a tuple
(A,\lambda,i,\eta,\tau)
to the tuple
(A,\lambda,i\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{F},(p)},\gamma^{-1}}\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{F},(p)},\eta,\gamma(\tau))
, here
\tau:\Lambda^{\vee}_{F}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{S}\xrightarrow{\sim}R^{1}h_{*}\Omega^{*}_{A/S}
and we define
\gamma(\tau)
to be the composition
\tau\circ(\iota_{\gamma}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{S})
where
\iota_{\gamma}:\Lambda^{\vee}_{F}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{\mathsf{F},(p)},\gamma^{-1}}\mathcal{O}_{F}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda^{\vee}_{F}
is the natural isomorphism arising from the trivial descent datum on
\Lambda_{V,F}\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
. By definition, this lifts the
\Gamma
action on the moduli problem
\mathscr{S}_{M_{p,F}}(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})
. One is left to check that the map
\pi_{F,V}
is
\Gamma
-equivariant, and that this moduli interpretation for the
\Gamma
action agrees with the naive description of the
\Gamma
-action on the Hilbert–Siegel modular varieties we consider by uniformization. We check the former, the latter is left to the reader. By flatness and normality of
\widetilde{\mathscr{S}}_{M_{p,F}}(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})
and its local model it suffices to check this over the generic fiber, but here we see that

\pi_{F,V,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}(\gamma(A,\lambda,i,\eta,\tau))=\iota_{\gamma}^{-1}\tau^{-1}(\mathcal{F}^{1}(R^{1}h_{*}\Omega^{*}_{A/S}))

which was exactly our definition of the
\Gamma
-action on the local model
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}_{V,F},\mu,\mathbb{Q}_{p}}
, see Remark
4.3.9
. ∎

4.3.12.

Finally, we state a Proposition which will be used in the proof of part (3) of Theorem
2
. The assumption that
\mathcal{H}
is hyperspecial can probably be relaxed with some effort; this assumption is however present for different reasons in part (3) of Theorem
2
.

Proposition 4.3.13
.

Assume that
\mathcal{H}
is the parahoric group scheme associated to a hyperspecial vertex in the Bruhat-Tits building of
H
. Let
x:\operatorname{Spec}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
be a point which in the image of the inclusion
\mathscr{S}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to\mathscr{S}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
. Then there exists a
\Gamma
-fixed point
y:\operatorname{Spec}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}\to\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H},\mu}
and a
\Gamma
-equivariant isomorphism of tangent spaces

\Theta:T_{x}(\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})})\to T_{y}(\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H},\mu}).

Proof.

We actually prove slightly more. We will borrow freely the notation of Proposition
4.3.11
. Let
x
be as above and let
R_{x}
denote the complete local ring of
\mathscr{S}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
at
x
. We let
S_{x}
denote the versal deformation ring of the p-divisible group with
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-action living over the point
x
. By the argument of
[
17
, Proposition 2.3.5]
, the natural morphism
S_{x}\to R_{x}
factors through a surjection onto the complete local ring of some point
y\in\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mu}
, we denote this ring by
S_{y}
. Let
x^{\prime}
denote
\iota_{H_{1}}(x)
, let
y^{\prime}=\iota_{H_{1},p}(y)
and let
R^{\prime}_{x^{\prime}},S^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}}
the complete local rings of
\mathscr{S}_{M_{p,F}}(\mathsf{G}_{V,\mathsf{F}},\mathsf{H}_{V,\mathsf{F}})
,
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}_{V,F},\mu}
at these points. By construction of the map
S_{x}\to R_{x}
and by the results of Proposition
4.3.11
, one has a commutative diagram of morphisms of local rings

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S_{y}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R_{x}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\prime}_{x^{\prime}}}

where the vertical arrows are surjections (for the right hand vertical arrow, this follows from on
[
37
, Theorem 1.1.1]
), and all maps are
\Gamma
-equivariant. Since the maps on Zariski tangent spaces induced by the vertical arrows are injective, it thus suffices to deduce
\Gamma
-equivariance of the lower horizontal arrow, so we are reduced to the Hilbert–Siegel case.

Let
\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}},\bar{S^{\prime}}_{y^{\prime}}
denote the special fiber of the deformation rings
R^{\prime}_{x^{\prime}},S^{\prime}_{y^{\prime}}
respectively. The induced map
\bar{S^{\prime}}_{y^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{y^{\prime}}^{p}\to\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}
has an explicit moduli theoretic interpretation in terms of Grothendieck–Messing’s crystalline deformation theory. We let
\mathcal{A}
denote the universal polarized abelian scheme with
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-action over
\operatorname{Spec}(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}})
, and let
\mathcal{A}_{0}
denote its fiber over the point
\operatorname{Spec}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})=\operatorname{Spec}(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}})
in what follows. The ring map
\bar{R}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}\to\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}=\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}
admits a set of trivial nilpotent divided powers, whence one has a canonical identification of polarized
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-modules (via the Gauss–Manin connection)
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}/\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}
, and one has a canonical identification
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{cris}}^{1}(\mathcal{A}_{0}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))
, and finally one has the identification
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))\otimes\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}=\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
. Because
x^{\prime}
is in the image of
\mathscr{S}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
, we may fix an
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-equivariant trivialization of
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}/\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Lambda^{\vee}_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
compatible with the standard symplectic pairing on
\Lambda^{\vee}_{F}
and the polarization on
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}/\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
, the
\Gamma
action, and such that the image of the Hodge filtration under this trivialization defines the point
y^{\prime}
on
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H}_{1},\mu}
. Now by the result
[
21
, Theorem V.1.10]
the image of the Hodge filtration
F^{0}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))
inside of
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))\simeq(\Lambda^{\vee}_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p})
gives the isomorphism
\bar{S^{\prime}}_{y^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{y^{\prime}}^{p}\to\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}
.

Now the action of
\Gamma
on the source takes the abelian scheme
\mathcal{A}
to
\gamma^{*}\mathcal{A}
. On the other hand as
\mathcal{A}_{0}
arises from an abelian variety
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{0}
such that
\mathcal{A}_{0}=\mathcal{A}^{\prime}_{0}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
and thus there exists a canonical isomorphism
\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}^{\prime}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))\otimes\mathcal{O}_{F}
compatible with the
\mathcal{O}_{F}
action and polarization, as well as the
\Gamma
action. There is a canonical isomorphism
\gamma^{*}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\gamma^{*}\mathcal{A}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))
of polarized
\mathcal{O}_{F}
-modules. Finally the desired
\Gamma
-equivariance follows from scrutinizing the commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\gamma^{*}\mathcal{A}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{dR}}(\mathcal{A}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\gamma^{*}\mathcal{A}_{0}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\mathcal{A}_{0}/(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gamma^{*}(\Lambda^{\vee}_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Lambda^{\vee}_{F}\otimes_{\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}\otimes_{\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}}(\bar{R^{\prime}}_{x^{\prime}}/\mathfrak{m}_{x^{\prime}}^{p}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{H}^{1}_{\mathrm{cris}}(\psi_{\gamma}^{-1})}

∎

Remark 4.3.14
.

We had originally hoped to prove the
\Gamma
-equivariance in Proposition
4.3.11
using the
\Gamma
-equivariance of shtukas and the interpretation of the local model diagram in terms of shtukas, see
[
24
, Section 4.9.1]
. This does not seem to be possible however, since the scheme-theoretic local model diagram is not uniquely pinned down by the v-sheaf theoretic local model diagram, as discussed in
[
24
, Section 4.9.1]
.

5.
Fixed points of integral models of Shimura varieties

In this section we prove Theorem
2
from the introduction.

5.1.
Fixed points of integral models of Shimura varieties of Hodge type

Let
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
be a Shimura datum of Hodge type, let
\mathsf{F}
be a totally real Galois extension of
\mathbb{Q}
with Galois group
\Gamma
. Let
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}\to{(\mathsf{H},\mathsf{Y})}
be as in Section
3.6
. We let
\mathcal{G}^{\circ}
be a parahoric model of
G
that is the identity component of a Bruhat–Tits stabilizer group scheme
\mathcal{G}
corresponding to some point
x
in the Bruhat–Tits building of
G(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We let
\mathcal{H}^{\circ}\subset\mathcal{H}
be the corresponding objects for
H
, and we let
K_{p}=\mathcal{H}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
K_{p}^{\circ}=\mathcal{H}^{\circ}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. We let
\mathcal{H}_{1}
be the Bruhat–Tits stabilizer group scheme for
H_{1}
and we let
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{H}_{1}
be the inverse image of
\mathcal{H}^{\circ}\subset\mathcal{H}
. We let
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}
be the Bruhat–Tits stabilizer group scheme corresponding to the image of
x
in the building of
G^{\mathrm{ad}}
, and similarly we define
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}
. We also need the parahoric group schemes
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}
and
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}
, and we observe that

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}\simeq\operatorname{Res}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}},\qquad\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}\simeq\operatorname{Res}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}}\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}_{\mathcal{O}_{F}}.

(5.1.1)

We set
K_{p,1}=\mathcal{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=K_{p}\cap H_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
and
K_{p,1}^{\prime}=\mathcal{H}_{1}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})=K_{p}^{\prime}\cap H_{1}(\mathbb{Q}_{p})
. We also have
K_{p}^{\Gamma}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
and
K_{p}^{\circ,\Gamma}=\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{Z}_{p})
.

Remark 5.1.1
.

The notation just introduced conflicts slightly with that of the introduction. The objects denoted by
\mathcal{G},\mathcal{H}_{1}
in the introduction correspond to
\mathcal{G}^{\circ},\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}
in this section.

In what follows we let
K^{p}\subset\mathsf{H}(\mathbb{A}_{f}^{p})
denote a neat good compact open subgroup such that there is a prime number
\ell\not=p
coprime to the order of
\Gamma
such that
K^{p}=K^{p,\ell}K_{\ell}
with
K_{\ell}
a pro-
\ell
group. For such
K^{p}
we have
K_{1},K_{1}^{\prime},K^{\Gamma},K^{\circ,\Gamma}
defined in the obvious way using
K_{1}^{p}
and
K^{p,\Gamma}
. Note that if
\mathsf{F}
is tamely ramified over
\mathbb{Q}
, then such
K^{p}
form a cofinal collection of compact open subgroups by Proposition
3.4.7
. Note that there are integral models of levels
K_{1},K_{1}^{\prime},K^{\Gamma},K^{\circ,\Gamma}
satisfying the Pappas–Rapoport axioms. These agree, by construction, with the ones of
[
16
]
that we used in Section
4.3
. The following theorem generalizes Theorem
2
.

Theorem 5.1.2
.

Assume that
p
is unramified in
\mathsf{F}
, that
p>2
and that
\Sha^{1}(\mathbb{Q},\mathsf{G})\to\Sha^{1}(\mathsf{F},\mathsf{G})
.

(1)

The natural map

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ,\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}.

(5.1.2)

is a universal homeomorphism.

(2)

If
p
is coprime to
|\Gamma|\cdot|\pi_{1}(G^{\mathrm{der}})|
and
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
satisfies Hypothesis
4.3.2
, then the natural map of (
5.1.2
) is an isomorphism.

(3)

If
K_{p}
is hyperspecial, then the natural map of (
5.1.2
) is an isomorphism.

Proof.

Part (1):
We first observe that the natural map
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}
factors through
\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}
because
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}\to\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}
factors through
\mathcal{H}
, and
\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime}
maps to
\mathcal{H}^{\circ}
. The corresponding map
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\lozenge/}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime},\mu}
is
\Gamma
-equivariant in the
2
-categorical sense by Corollary
4.2.2
, and so is its composition with
\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime},\mu}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu}
.

By Lemma
2.6.6
, using the fact that
p
is unramified in
\mathsf{F}
, there is an isomorphism

\displaystyle\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu}\to\left(\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu}\right)^{h\Gamma}.

(5.1.3)

This induces a map

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\lozenge/,\Gamma}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu},

(5.1.4)

compatible with the maps
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ,\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}^{\lozenge/}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\circ},\mu}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu}
and
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ,\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}^{\lozenge/}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\lozenge/,\Gamma}
. For
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}(\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p})
we let
b_{x}:\operatorname{Spd}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu}
be the corresponding
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}
-shtuka. Then by Lemma
4.2.4
, there is a morphism

\displaystyle\Psi_{b_{x}}:\left(\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}\right)_{/x}^{\lozenge}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},b_{x},\mu,/x_{0}},

(5.1.5)

and an isomorphism from the pullback under
\Psi_{b_{x}}
of the tautological
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}
-shtuka on
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},b_{x},\mu,/x_{0}}
to the universal
\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ}
-shtuka pulled back from
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma,\lozenge/}
. If we let
d_{x}
denote the induced map
\operatorname{Spd}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},\mu}
, then by the uniqueness proved in Lemma
4.2.4
, there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}\right)_{/x}^{\lozenge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},b_{x},\mu,/x_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\left(\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}\right)_{/x}^{\lozenge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},b_{x},\mu,/x_{0}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{b_{x}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Psi_{d_{x}}}

(5.1.6)

where the vertical arrows are the natural closed immersions. We note that the map
\Psi_{d_{x}}
is an isomorphism by Corollary
4.2.5
and the fact that (for a lift
b_{x}^{\prime}:\operatorname{Spd}\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}\to\operatorname{Sht}_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime},\mu}
of
b_{x}
)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{H}_{1}^{\prime},b_{x}^{\prime},\mu,/x_{0}}\to\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},b_{x},\mu,/x_{0}}

(5.1.7)

is an isomorphism, see
[
25
, Theorem 5.1.2]
. It is moreover a direct consequence of the uniqueness proved in Lemma
4.2.4
that the morphism
\Psi_{d_{x}}
is
\Gamma
-equivariant. Since taking
\Gamma
-invariants commutes with taking diamond functors and formal completions, we see using Proposition
2.6.10
that
\Psi_{b_{x}}
is the
\Gamma
-fixed points of
\Psi_{d_{x}}
. In particular,
\Psi_{b_{x}}
is an isomorphism.

By
[
25
, Theorem 2.5.4, Theorem 2.5.5]
in combination with
[
9
, Corollary 1.4]
, using our assumption that
p>2
, there is a flat normal formal scheme
\mathcal{N}
such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}^{\lozenge}\simeq\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},b_{x},\mu,/x_{0}}.

(5.1.8)

In particular, the unique
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-point of
\mathcal{M}^{\mathrm{int}}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad},\circ},b_{x},\mu,/x_{0}}
(and thus of
\left(\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}\right)_{/x}
) lifts to a
\operatorname{Spf}L
point for a finite extension
L
of
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. Since this is true for all
x
, this implies that
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}
is topologically flat over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
, i.e., that its generic fiber is dense. Let
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{awn}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}
be the absolute weak normalization, see
[
32
, Lemma 0EUS]
; this is a universal homeomorphism and so
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{awn}}
is again topologically flat over
\mathcal{O}_{E}
. Then by
[
1
, Lemma 2.13]
, the natural map

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{awn}}\right)_{/x}^{\lozenge}\to\left(\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}\right)_{/x}^{\lozenge}

(5.1.9)

is an isomorphism. By the fully faithfulness of the
\lozenge
functor on absolute weakly normal formal schemes flat separated and topologically of finite type over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, see
[
1
, Theorem 2.16]
, we find that

\displaystyle\left(\widehat{\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{awn}}\right)_{/x}\simeq\mathcal{N}.

(5.1.10)

This implies that the complete local rings of
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{awn}}
at
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
points are normal, and thus that the local rings are normal by
[
32
, Lemma 0FIZ]
. This shows that
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{awn}}
is normal, because normality (of a quasicompact scheme) can be checked at closed points. By the universal property of the absolute weak normalization, the natural map
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ,\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}
lifts to a finite map

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ,\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{awn}}.

(5.1.11)

This map is an isomorphism on the generic fiber by Theorem
3.6.4
and thus an isomorphism since the target is normal and source and target are flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
, see
[
32
, Lemma 0AB1]
. The theorem now follows from the fact that
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma,\mathrm{awn}}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}
is a universal homeomorphism.

Part (2):
We assume from now on that
p
is coprime to the order of
\Gamma
and to the order of
\pi_{1}(G^{\mathrm{der}})
, and that
{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X},\mathcal{G})}
satisfies Assumption
4.3.2
. It then follows from Proposition
4.3.11
that there is a
\Gamma
-equivariant and smooth map

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}\to\left[\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mu}/\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}\right].

(5.1.12)

It follows from
[
7
, Proposition 4.2]
, using the fact that
\Gamma
is of order prime-to-
p
, that the induced map

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}\to\left[\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mu}/\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}\right]^{h\Gamma}.

(5.1.13)

is also smooth. Recall that Corollary
2.4.9
tells us that the natural map

\displaystyle\left[\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mu}/\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}}\right]\to\left[\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mu}/\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}}\right]^{h\Gamma}

(5.1.14)

is an isomorphism. Thus
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}
has a smooth local model diagram to
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mu}
and is thus flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and normal since
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mu}
is, see
[
9
, Corollary 1.4]
. The natural map
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
is
\Gamma
-equivariant (by
[
4
, Corollary 4.1.10]
) and finite étale (by the proof of
[
4
, Theorem 4.1.12]
) and thus the induced map on fixed points

\displaystyle\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}

(5.1.15)

is also finite étale. It follows that
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}
is flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and normal since
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}
is. Now the natural map of the theorem is a finite morphism that induces an isomorphism on generic fibers by Theorem
3.6.4
. Since the target is flat over
\mathbb{Z}_{p}
and normal, it follows from
[
32
, Lemma 0AB1]
that the map is an isomorphism.

Part (3):
We have a diagram

\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ,\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}.

We first observe that the composite is a closed immersion by
[
37
, Theorem 1.1.1]
and the second arrow is a closed immersion because
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}
is separated; it follows that the first arrow is a closed immersion. From part (1), it moreover follows that the first arrow is a universal homeomorphism. To show that the first map is an isomorphism, it is thus enough to show it induces isomorphisms on complete local rings of
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-points, see e.g.
[
32
, Lemma 0819]
. By Lemma
5.1.3
below, using the fact that
K_{p}^{\circ,\Gamma}
is hyperspecial, it suffices to show that the induced map on tangent spaces at
\overline{\mathbb{F}}_{p}
-points is a bijection. Since the map is a closed immersion, it suffices to show that for
x\in\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ,\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
the tangent space of
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ,\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}\to\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}
at
x
has the same dimension as the tangent space of
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}
at
x
.

We note that
\mathscr{S}_{K^{\circ,\Gamma}}{(\mathsf{G},\mathsf{X})}
is smooth, and that the tangent space at
x
has dimension equal to the dimension of the tangent space of any point in
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G},\mu}=\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mu}
. By Proposition
4.3.13
, we may identify the tangent space of
\mathscr{S}_{K_{1}^{\prime}}{(\mathsf{H}_{1},\mathsf{Y}_{1})}^{\Gamma}
at
x
with the
\Gamma
-fixed point of the tangent space of
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H},\mu}
at a
\Gamma
-fixed point
y
of
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H},\mu}=\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{H}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mu}
. By Proposition
2.4.8
, we have
y\in\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mu}
, and moreover the
\Gamma
-fixed points of the tangent space gives the tangent space of
\mathbb{M}_{\mathcal{G}^{\mathrm{ad}},\mu}
at
y
. This proves the claim about the dimensions of tangent spaces, and so we are done by the argument in the previous paragraph.
∎

Lemma 5.1.3
.

Let
f:A\to B
be a surjective local homomorphism of complete local Noetherian
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
-algebras with
B
flat, formally smooth, and unramified over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
. Suppose that
f
induces a universal homeomorphism on spectra. If
f
induces an isomorphism on residue fields and tangent spaces, then it is an isomorphism.

Proof.

The proof is no doubt well known to experts, but we were unable to find a suitable reference. Because
B
is formally smooth over
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}
we have that it is a complete local ring of the form
\breve{\mathbb{Z}}_{p}[[X_{1},\dots,X_{n}]]
. The map
f:A\to B
admits a section
g:B\to A
, which we may construct by taking
g(X_{i})
to be any
Y\in A
such that
f(Y)=X_{i}
. The morphism
g
is finite, as both
A,B
are complete and
g
induces an isomorphism on residue fields and tangent spaces. Thus
g\circ f
makes
A
a finite
A
algebra, and so by Nakayama’s lemma we see that
g\circ f
is a surjection, as
A
is Noetherian; it is thus an isomorphism.
∎

Remark 5.1.4
.

One can prove a version of Part (1) of Theorem
5.1.2
for integral models of Shimura varieties of abelian type
